## Idea

An endomap `f : A → A` is
{{#concept "split idempotent" Disambiguation="map of types" Agda=is-split-idempotent}}
if there is a type `B` and an inclusion-retraction pair `i , r` displaying `B`
as a [retract](foundation-core.retracts-of-types.md) of `A`, and such that
`H : i ∘ r ~ f`. In other words, if we have a commutative diagram

```text
           f
       A ----> A
      ∧ \     ∧
   i /   \r  / i
    /     ∨ /
  B ====== B.
```

Observe that split idempotent maps are indeed
[idempotent](foundation.idempotent-maps.md), as witnessed by the composite

```text
       (H◽H)⁻¹            iRr        H
  f ∘ f  ~  i ∘ r ∘ i ∘ r  ~  i ∘ r  ~  f
```

where `H : i ∘ r ~ f` and `R : r ∘ i ~ id`.

One important fact about split idempotent maps is that every
[quasicoherently idempotent map](foundation.quasicoherently-idempotent-maps.md)
splits, and conversely, every split idempotent map is quasicoherently
idempotent. In fact, the type of proofs of split idempotence for an endomap `f`
is a retract of the type of proofs of quasicoherent idempotence. {{#cite Shu17}}

### The type of split idempotent maps on a type

### The splitting type of a split idempotent map is essentially unique

This is Lemma 3.4 in {{#cite Shu17}}. Note that it does not require any
postulates.

**Proof.** Given two splittings of an endomap `f : A → A`, with
inclusion-retraction pairs `i , r` and `i' , r'`, we get mutual inverse maps
between the splitting types

```text
  r' ∘ i : B → B'    and    r ∘ i' : B' → B.
```

The computation that they form mutual inverses is straightforward:

```text
                   rR'i        R
  r ∘ i' ∘ r' ∘ i   ~   r ∘ i  ~  id.
```

### The type of split idempotent maps on `A` is equivalent to retracts of `A`

Note that the proof relies on the
[function extensionality](foundation.function-extensionality.md) axiom.

<pre class="Agda"><a id="11302" class="Keyword">module</a> <a id="11309" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#11309" class="Module">_</a>

### Characterizing equality of split idempotence structures

We characterize equality of witnesses that an endomap `f` is split idempotent as
equivalences of splitting types such that the evident retracts are homotopic. In
particular, this characterization relies on the univalence axiom.

### Split idempotent maps are idempotent

This is Lemma 3.3 in {{#cite Shu17}}.

**Proof.** Given a split idempotent map `f` with splitting `R : r ∘ i ~ id` and
homotopy `H : i ∘ r ~ f`, then `f` is idempotent via the following witness

```text
       (H◽H)⁻¹            iRr        H
  f ∘ f  ~  i ∘ r ∘ i ∘ r  ~  i ∘ r  ~  f.
```

### Split idempotent maps are quasicoherently idempotent

This is Lemma 3.6 in {{#cite Shu17}}.

**Proof.** Inclusion-retraction composites are quasicoherently idempotent, so
since quasicoherently idempotent maps are closed under homotopy we are done.

  <a id="17856" class="Keyword">where</a>

  <a id="17865" class="Keyword">abstract</a>

### Every idempotent map on a set splits

This is Theorem 3.7 of {{#cite Shu17}}.

This is Theorem 3.9 of {{#cite Shu17}}.

    <a id="23933" class="Symbol">(</a> <a id="23935" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#21588" class="Function">splitting-type-is-split-idempotent-is-weakly-constant-map-is-idempotent</a> <a id="24007" href="foundation.dependent-pair-types.html#787" class="InductiveConstructor Operator">,</a>
      <a id="24015" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#23088" class="Function">retract-is-split-idempotent-is-weakly-constant-map-is-idempotent</a> <a id="24080" href="foundation.dependent-pair-types.html#787" class="InductiveConstructor Operator">,</a>
      <a id="24088" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#23481" class="Function">htpy-is-split-idempotent-is-weakly-constant-map-is-idempotent</a><a id="24149" class="Symbol">)</a>
</pre>
### Quasicoherently idempotent maps split

This is Theorem 5.3 of {{#cite Shu17}}.

As per Remark 5.4 {{#cite Shu17}}, the inclusion of `A` into the splitting type
can be constructed for any endofunction `f`.

<pre class="Agda"><a id="24374" class="Keyword">module</a> <a id="24381" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24381" class="Module">_</a>
  <a id="24385" class="Symbol">{</a><a id="24386" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24386" class="Bound">l</a> <a id="24388" class="Symbol">:</a> <a id="24390" href="Agda.Primitive.html#742" class="Postulate">Level</a><a id="24395" class="Symbol">}</a> <a id="24397" class="Symbol">{</a><a id="24398" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24398" class="Bound">A</a> <a id="24400" class="Symbol">:</a> <a id="24402" href="Agda.Primitive.html#388" class="Primitive">UU</a> <a id="24405" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24386" class="Bound">l</a><a id="24406" class="Symbol">}</a> <a id="24408" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="24409" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24409" class="Bound">f</a> <a id="24411" class="Symbol">:</a> <a id="24413" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24398" class="Bound">A</a> <a id="24415" class="Symbol">→</a> <a id="24417" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24398" class="Bound">A</a><a id="24418" class="Symbol">)</a>
  <a id="24422" class="Keyword">where</a>

  <a id="24431" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24431" class="Function">inverse-sequential-diagram-splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent&#39;</a> <a id="24504" class="Symbol">:</a>
    <a id="24510" href="foundation.inverse-sequential-diagrams.html#1208" class="Function">inverse-sequential-diagram</a> <a id="24537" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24386" class="Bound">l</a>
  <a id="24541" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24431" class="Function">inverse-sequential-diagram-splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent&#39;</a> <a id="24614" class="Symbol">=</a>
    <a id="24620" class="Symbol">(</a> <a id="24622" class="Symbol">(λ</a> <a id="24625" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24625" class="Bound">_</a> <a id="24627" class="Symbol">→</a> <a id="24629" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24398" class="Bound">A</a><a id="24630" class="Symbol">)</a> <a id="24632" href="foundation.dependent-pair-types.html#787" class="InductiveConstructor Operator">,</a> <a id="24634" class="Symbol">(λ</a> <a id="24637" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24637" class="Bound">_</a> <a id="24639" class="Symbol">→</a> <a id="24641" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24409" class="Bound">f</a><a id="24642" class="Symbol">))</a>

  <a id="24648" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24648" class="Function">splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent&#39;</a> <a id="24694" class="Symbol">:</a> <a id="24696" href="Agda.Primitive.html#388" class="Primitive">UU</a> <a id="24699" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24386" class="Bound">l</a>
  <a id="24703" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24648" class="Function">splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent&#39;</a> <a id="24749" class="Symbol">=</a>
    <a id="24755" href="foundation.sequential-limits.html#2101" class="Function">standard-sequential-limit</a>
      <a id="24787" class="Symbol">(</a> <a id="24789" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24431" class="Function">inverse-sequential-diagram-splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent&#39;</a><a id="24861" class="Symbol">)</a>

  <a id="24866" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24866" class="Function">inclusion-splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent&#39;</a> <a id="24922" class="Symbol">:</a>
    <a id="24928" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24648" class="Function">splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent&#39;</a> <a id="24974" class="Symbol">→</a> <a id="24976" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24398" class="Bound">A</a>
  <a id="24980" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24866" class="Function">inclusion-splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent&#39;</a> <a id="25036" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="25037" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25037" class="Bound">a</a> <a id="25039" href="foundation.dependent-pair-types.html#787" class="InductiveConstructor Operator">,</a> <a id="25041" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25041" class="Bound">α</a><a id="25042" class="Symbol">)</a> <a id="25044" class="Symbol">=</a> <a id="25046" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25037" class="Bound">a</a> <a id="25048" class="Number">0</a>
</pre>
Moreover, again by Remark 5.4 {{#cite Shu17}}, given only the idempotence
homotopy `f ∘ f ~ f`, we can construct the converse map to this inclusion and
show that this gives a factorization of `f`. Indeed, this factorization is
strict.

<pre class="Agda"><a id="25299" class="Keyword">module</a> <a id="25306" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25306" class="Module">_</a>
  <a id="25310" class="Symbol">{</a><a id="25311" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25311" class="Bound">l</a> <a id="25313" class="Symbol">:</a> <a id="25315" href="Agda.Primitive.html#742" class="Postulate">Level</a><a id="25320" class="Symbol">}</a> <a id="25322" class="Symbol">{</a><a id="25323" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25323" class="Bound">A</a> <a id="25325" class="Symbol">:</a> <a id="25327" href="Agda.Primitive.html#388" class="Primitive">UU</a> <a id="25330" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25311" class="Bound">l</a><a id="25331" class="Symbol">}</a> <a id="25333" class="Symbol">{</a><a id="25334" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25334" class="Bound">f</a> <a id="25336" class="Symbol">:</a> <a id="25338" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25323" class="Bound">A</a> <a id="25340" class="Symbol">→</a> <a id="25342" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25323" class="Bound">A</a><a id="25343" class="Symbol">}</a>
  <a id="25347" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="25348" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25348" class="Bound">I</a> <a id="25350" class="Symbol">:</a> <a id="25352" href="foundation.idempotent-maps.html#2443" class="Function">is-idempotent</a> <a id="25366" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25334" class="Bound">f</a><a id="25367" class="Symbol">)</a>
  <a id="25371" class="Keyword">where</a>

  <a id="25380" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25380" class="Function">map-retraction-splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent&#39;</a> <a id="25441" class="Symbol">:</a>
    <a id="25447" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25323" class="Bound">A</a> <a id="25449" class="Symbol">→</a> <a id="25451" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24648" class="Function">splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent&#39;</a> <a id="25497" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25334" class="Bound">f</a>
  <a id="25501" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25380" class="Function">map-retraction-splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent&#39;</a> <a id="25562" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25562" class="Bound">x</a> <a id="25564" class="Symbol">=</a>
    <a id="25570" class="Symbol">(</a> <a id="25572" class="Symbol">(λ</a> <a id="25575" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25575" class="Bound">_</a> <a id="25577" class="Symbol">→</a> <a id="25579" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25334" class="Bound">f</a> <a id="25581" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25562" class="Bound">x</a><a id="25582" class="Symbol">)</a> <a id="25584" href="foundation.dependent-pair-types.html#787" class="InductiveConstructor Operator">,</a> <a id="25586" class="Symbol">(λ</a> <a id="25589" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25589" class="Bound">_</a> <a id="25591" class="Symbol">→</a> <a id="25593" href="foundation-core.identity-types.html#6358" class="Function">inv</a> <a id="25597" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="25598" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25348" class="Bound">I</a> <a id="25600" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25562" class="Bound">x</a><a id="25601" class="Symbol">)))</a>

  <a id="25608" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25608" class="Function">htpy-is-split-idempotent-is-quasicoherently-idempotent&#39;</a> <a id="25664" class="Symbol">:</a>
    <a id="25670" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24866" class="Function">inclusion-splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent&#39;</a> <a id="25726" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#25334" class="Bound">f</a> <a id="25728" href="foundation-core.function-types.html#504" class="Function Operator">∘</a>

However, to show that these maps form an inclusion-retraction pair, we use the
coherence of quasicoherent idempotents as well as the function extensionality
axiom.

  <a id="26139" class="Keyword">where</a>

Now, to construct the desired retracting homotopy

```text
  r ∘ i ~ id
```

we subdivide the problem into two. First, we show that shifting the sequence and
whiskering by `f ∘ f` is homotopic to the identity

```text
  ((f (f a₍₋₎₊₁)) , (f ∘ f) ·l α₍₋₎₊₁) ~ (a , α).
```

<pre class="Agda">  <a id="27766" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#27766" class="Function">shift-retraction-splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent</a> <a id="27828" class="Symbol">:</a>
    <a id="27834" href="foundation.sequential-limits.html#2101" class="Function">standard-sequential-limit</a>
      <a id="27866" class="Symbol">(</a> <a id="27868" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#26148" class="Function">inverse-sequential-diagram-splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent</a><a id="27939" class="Symbol">)</a> <a id="27941" class="Symbol">→</a>
    <a id="27947" href="foundation.sequential-limits.html#2101" class="Function">standard-sequential-limit</a>
      <a id="27979" class="Symbol">(</a> <a id="27981" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#26148" class="Function">inverse-sequential-diagram-splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent</a><a id="28052" class="Symbol">)</a>
  <a id="28056" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#27766" class="Function">shift-retraction-splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent</a> <a id="28118" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="28119" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#28119" class="Bound">a</a> <a id="28121" href="foundation.dependent-pair-types.html#787" class="InductiveConstructor Operator">,</a> <a id="28123" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#28123" class="Bound">α</a><a id="28124" class="Symbol">)</a> <a id="28126" class="Symbol">=</a>
    <a id="28132" class="Symbol">((</a><a id="28134" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#26086" class="Bound">f</a> <a id="28136" href="foundation-core.function-types.html#504" class="Function Operator">∘</a> <a id="28138" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#26086" class="Bound">f</a> <a id="28140" href="foundation-core.function-types.html#504" class="Function Operator">∘</a> <a id="28142" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#28119" class="Bound">a</a> <a id="28144" href="foundation-core.function-types.html#504" class="Function Operator">∘</a> <a id="28146" href="elementary-number-theory.natural-numbers.html#859" class="InductiveConstructor">succ-ℕ</a><a id="28152" class="Symbol">)</a> <a id="28154" href="foundation.dependent-pair-types.html#787" class="InductiveConstructor Operator">,</a> <a id="28156" class="Symbol">(</a> <a id="28158" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="28159" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#26086" class="Bound">f</a> <a id="28161" href="foundation-core.function-types.html#504" class="Function Operator">∘</a> <a id="28163" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#26086" class="Bound">f</a><a id="28164" class="Symbol">)</a> <a id="28166" href="foundation.whiskering-homotopies-composition.html#2364" class="Function Operator">·l</a> <a id="28169" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="28170" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#28123" class="Bound">α</a> <a id="28172" href="foundation-core.function-types.html#504" class="Function Operator">∘</a> <a id="28174" href="elementary-number-theory.natural-numbers.html#859" class="InductiveConstructor">succ-ℕ</a><a id="28180" class="Symbol">)))</a>

  <a id="28187" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#28187" class="Function">htpy-sequence-shift-retraction-splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent</a> <a id="28263" class="Symbol">:</a>
    <a id="28269" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="28270" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#28270" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="28271" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#28271" class="Bound">a</a> <a id="28273" href="foundation.dependent-pair-types.html#787" class="InductiveConstructor Operator">,</a> <a id="28275" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#28275" class="Bound">α</a><a id="28276" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#28270" class="Symbol">)</a> <a id="28278" class="Symbol">:</a>

Then we show that `r ∘ i` is homotopic to this operation. This time we proceed
by induction on `n`.

For the inductive step we fill the following diagram as prescribed, in the
notation of {{#cite Shu17}}:

```text
                  ξₙ₊₁                   I aₙ₊₁
        f a₀ ------------> f (f aₙ₊₁) -------------> f aₙ₊₁
         |                     |       [nat-htpy]      |
  I⁻¹ a₀ |        [Ξₙ]         |                       | f (αₙ₊₁)⁻¹
         ∨                     ∨        -------->      ∨
      f (f a₀) --------> f (f (f aₙ₊₂))    [J]    f (f aₙ₊₂) ----> f (f (f aₙ₊₃))
         (f (ξₙ ∙ I aₙ₊₁ ∙ f αₙ₊₁))     -------->          f (f αₙ₊₂)
                                        f (I aₙ₊₂)
```

where the symbols translate to code as follows:

```text
I = is-idempotent-is-quasicoherently-idempotent H
J = coh-is-quasicoherently-idempotent H
ξ = htpy-sequence-compute-retraction-splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent (a , α)
Ξ = htpy-coherence-compute-retraction-splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent (a , α).
```

Note, in particular, that the left-hand square is the inductive hypothesis.

### If a map is split idempotent at any universe level, it is split idempotent at its own universe level

### Small types are closed under retracts

<!-- TODO: move this somewhere more fitting. Currently moving it to foundation.small-types introduces cyclic dependencies -->

This is Theorem 2.13 of {{#cite dJE23}}. Note, in particular, that it does not
rely on univalence.

**Proof:** Assume given an inclusion-retraction pair `i , r` that displays `B`
as a retract of the small type `A`. By essential uniqueness of splitting types,
`B` is equivalent to every other splitting type of `i ∘ r`. Now, another
splitting type of `i ∘ r` is the splitting type as constructed for the witness

```text
  is-split-idempotent-is-quasicoherently-idempotent
    ( is-quasicoherently-idempotent-inclusion-retraction i r ...),
```

and this is a small sequential limit. Hence `B` is equivalent to a small type,
and so is itself small.

<pre class="Agda"><a id="44219" class="Keyword">module</a> <a id="44226" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#44226" class="Module">_</a>
  <a id="44230" class="Symbol">{</a><a id="44231" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#44231" class="Bound">l1</a> <a id="44234" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#44234" class="Bound">l2</a> <a id="44237" class="Symbol">:</a> <a id="44239" href="Agda.Primitive.html#742" class="Postulate">Level</a><a id="44244" class="Symbol">}</a> <a id="44246" class="Symbol">{</a><a id="44247" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#44247" class="Bound">A</a> <a id="44249" class="Symbol">:</a> <a id="44251" href="Agda.Primitive.html#388" class="Primitive">UU</a> <a id="44254" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#44231" class="Bound">l1</a><a id="44256" class="Symbol">}</a> <a id="44258" class="Symbol">{</a><a id="44259" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#44259" class="Bound">B</a> <a id="44261" class="Symbol">:</a> <a id="44263" href="Agda.Primitive.html#388" class="Primitive">UU</a> <a id="44266" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#44234" class="Bound">l2</a><a id="44268" class="Symbol">}</a>
  <a id="44272" class="Keyword">where</a>

  <a id="44281" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#44281" class="Function">is-small-retract&#39;</a> <a id="44299" class="Symbol">:</a> <a id="44301" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#44259" class="Bound">B</a> <a id="44303" href="foundation-core.retracts-of-types.html#1754" class="Function Operator">retract-of</a> <a id="44314" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#44247" class="Bound">A</a> <a id="44316" class="Symbol">→</a> <a id="44318" href="foundation-core.small-types.html#1494" class="Function">is-small</a> <a id="44327" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#44231" class="Bound">l1</a> <a id="44330" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#44259" class="Bound">B</a>
  <a id="44334" href="foundation.dependent-pair-types.html#681" class="Field">pr1</a> <a id="44338" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="44339" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#44281" class="Function">is-small-retract&#39;</a> <a id="44357" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#44357" class="Bound">R</a><a id="44358" class="Symbol">)</a> <a id="44360" class="Symbol">=</a>
    <a id="44366" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#24648" class="Function">splitting-type-is-quasicoherently-idempotent&#39;</a>
      <a id="44418" class="Symbol">(</a> <a id="44420" href="foundation-core.retracts-of-types.html#1928" class="Function">inclusion-retract</a> <a id="44438" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#44357" class="Bound">R</a> <a id="44440" href="foundation-core.function-types.html#504" class="Function Operator">∘</a> <a id="44442" href="foundation-core.retracts-of-types.html#2067" class="Function">map-retraction-retract</a> <a id="44465" href="foundation.split-idempotent-maps.html#44357" class="Bound">R</a><a id="44466" class="Symbol">)</a>
